## Numberphile: repair the zero-exponent remainder proof (2026-10-29)

Local attribution: grwtsk contributors. Actual managed Noe replay of math
`2f23e7a7d53c514789a8d1afb0578e6e40a08004` reached the first source module and
failed at the zero-exponent case of the private `pow_remainder` helper:
`rfl` did not reduce integer powers of zero. The successor replaces that tactic
with `simp only [Int.pow_zero]`, using the exact Lean-core theorem rather than
assuming definitional reduction. No statement, hypothesis, axiom policy,
dependency pin or root import changed.

- **Current source and blob:** `GRWTSK/NumberTheory/SumsOfThreeCubes.lean`,

  The original fourteen public interfaces and twelve casting-out-nines interfaces
  remain the intended checks. Byte-identical current companion modules are
  `GRWTSK/NumberTheory/Taxicab.lean` (blob
  `1b87eb53e9d2445a6d46c530f99d040bc5aab6f6`) and
  `GRWTSK/NumberTheory/CastingOutNines.lean` (blob
  `ed5cd7224be30778fb6fa501751bb43ea18f1da5`).
- **Retained actual failure:** Noe runtime
  `formalization/numberphile-r3/run-1/receipt.json`, SHA-256
  `8d3cf15aeff77fb72a19dc2ecea2ec2bc64a145e289c8ff7c536fae8e00f8b21`; `passed=false`, `contracts=[]`,
  `host_config_restored=true`. The source was compiled through the managed
  trust-zero owner. Error-recovery output mentions `sorryAx` in dependent
  declarations after the failed helper; those declarations are rejected, not
  accepted with an exception. Printed empty-axiom reports for some independent
  identities do not turn the failed module into an accepted partial batch.
- **Evidence roles:** the failure is actual source-bound elaboration evidence.
  The repair is a source proof candidate using imported Lean-core arithmetic.
  No newly kernel-verified bridge is claimed yet. Finite identities, conditional
  interfaces, conjectures, video source bylines and missing mathematical bridges

## Numberphile: core-arithmetic successor and casting out nines (2026-10-28)

Local attribution: grwtsk contributors. The fourteen public cube/taxicab theorem
interfaces from math `16d655f2c5662cd3b94e4cbb122298f23343df15` are retained, with
explicit Lean-core proofs replacing the large Mathlib tactic dependency closure.
The original prepared Noe registry had 798 source modules. This is a source-level
proof simplification, not a claim that the original source passed verification.
No dependency version or root import changed. The private `pow_remainder` lemma
uses induction and `Int.mul_emod`; the universal cube result then transports each
integer into one of nine residues. Closed arithmetic proofs use kernel `decide`,
not `native_decide` or an external arithmetic oracle.

- **Successor source pins:**
  `GRWTSK/NumberTheory/DiophantineEquations/SumsOfThreeCubes.lean`, blob
  `d2ce432fa8c0b54e650aa7ea32ce076c0c9e5a03`;
  `GRWTSK/NumberTheory/DiophantineEquations/Taxicab.lean`, blob
  `1b87eb53e9d2445a6d46c530f99d040bc5aab6f6`.
  Their source titles, public bylines, dates, hypotheses, fourteen fully qualified
  public theorem names, explicit witnesses and mathematical limitations are those
  recorded in the first batch below. The containing publication commit pins the
  complete successor tree; earlier blob identities remain historical evidence.
- **New primary source:** Numberphile, *Casting Out Nines*, video by Brady Haran,
  featuring James Grime, 2017-09-10,
  https://www.numberphile.com/videos/casting-out-nines ;
  original video https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=FlndIiQa20o . The official page
  describes checking calculations. This entry formalizes the elementary method
  and its mathematical limits; complete timestamped video claim extraction is
  NOT_DONE. No claim is made that the video's exact examples are the local examples.
- **New source path/blob:**
  `GRWTSK/NumberTheory/ElementaryNumberTheory/CastingOutNines.lean`, blob
  `ed5cd7224be30778fb6fa501751bb43ea18f1da5`; containing publication commit pins
  the full source. Definitions `value` and `digitSum` take finite natural-number
  coefficient lists, least significant first. Coefficients below ten give ordinary
  decimal strings; the stronger congruence also holds for unrestricted natural
  coefficients, the empty list, zero and leading zeroes.
- **Exact method and consumer:** induction proves `value ds % 9 = digitSum ds % 9`.
  `AddCheck` and `MulCheck` compare reduced digit sums. They are equivalent to
  equality of numerical remainders, so a failed check certifies a wrong addition
  or multiplication. The converses are false: the incorrect answers `1+1=11` and
  `2*2=13` pass. For every natural `n,k`, the distinct number `n+9*(k+1)` has the
  same remainder as `n`. Swapping adjacent digits at any position, with arbitrary
  prefix and suffix, also preserves the checksum. These universal statements
  explain both the usefulness and the exact limitation of the checking method.
- **Fully qualified new declarations:** prefix
  `GRWTSK.NumberTheory.ElementaryNumberTheory.CastingOutNines.` with definitions
  `value`, `digitSum`, `AddCheck`, `MulCheck` and theorems `casting_out_nines`,
  `divisible_by_nine_iff`, `addCheck_iff`, `mulCheck_iff`,
  `correct_addition_passes`, `correct_multiplication_passes`,
  `failed_addition_check_detects_error`, `failed_multiplication_check_detects_error`,
  `addition_false_positive`, `multiplication_false_positive`,
  `undetectable_error_family`, `adjacent_transposition_undetectable`.
- **Evidence roles:** imported mathematics is Lean-core integer/natural arithmetic,
  finite lists and the proof-producing omega tactic. Kernel-verified bridges:
  none claimed at publication. Source proof candidates: the fourteen retained
  public results and twelve new public results, plus the transitive private
  remainder helper. Conditional interfaces: correctness hypotheses are explicit
  only in the two forward checking implications, and contraposition detects errors.
  Finite evidence: explicit counterexamples and cube identities. Analogies: none.
  Open premises: source elaboration, source-bound kernel/type checks and complete
  transitive axiom reports; three-cubes sufficiency and taxicab minimality remain
  unproved, and no entire video is marked complete.
- **Actual Noe attempt at the original source:** managed registry v3 preparation
  succeeded for all fourteen original theorem names at math `16d655f2c5662cd3b94e4cbb122298f23343df15`.
  Two one-shot launchd starts observed exit 78 before a supervisor start record;
  no replay-child result or Lean execution is inferred from them. The documented
  direct replay command then stopped with `BlockingIOError: [Errno 35] Resource
  temporarily unavailable` at the exclusive activation lock, held by the existing
  3Blue1Brown replay. Requests, registries, stderr, and the blocked-attempt summary
  are retained externally in Noe runtime `formalization/numberphile-r1/`.
  This is blocked admission, not a failed mathematical proof. No lock, reservation,
  host configuration or resource policy was cleared or changed by this request.
- **Verification status:** compilation NOT_RUN; fresh kernel checking NOT_RUN;
  transitive axiom auditing NOT_RUN for both the original and successor sources.
  The next managed request must pin the successor publication commit, all three
  source blobs, the current Noe producer, Lean toolchain and dependency lock. Audit

## Numberphile: first sums-of-cubes claim batch (2026-10-28)

Local attribution: grwtsk contributors. This is a partial claim formalization,
not completion of five videos or the entire channel. The passive scope manifest
`documentation/mathematics/_verification/specifications/_unscoped/numberphile-corpus-v1.json`
records 1,165 distinct official sitemap video-page URLs, the raw sitemap digest,
five partial claim mappings and explicit acceptance requirements. Descriptions
were consulted; complete video/transcript claim extraction remains NOT_DONE.
The sitemap is not a complete YouTube-channel inventory. No historical influence
or novelty is inferred from a topic match.

- **Primary video sources/bylines/versions:** Numberphile, videos by Brady Haran:
  *The Uncracked Problem with 33*, featuring Tim Browning (2015-11-06),
  https://www.numberphile.com/videos/the-uncracked-problem-with-33 ;
  *42 is the new 33*, featuring Andrew Booker (2019-03-11),
  https://www.numberphile.com/videos/42-is-the-new-33 ;
  *The Mystery of 42 is Solved*, featuring Andrew Booker (2019-09-06),
  https://www.numberphile.com/videos/the-mystery-of-42-is-solved ;
  *3 as the sum of the 3 cubes*, featuring Andrew Booker (2019-09-24),
  https://www.numberphile.com/videos/3-as-the-sum-of-the-3-cubes ;
  *1729 and Taxi Cabs*, featuring James Grime and Roger Bowley (2012-02-28),
  https://www.numberphile.com/videos/1729-and-taxi-cabs . Dates are the official
  pages' publication metadata. These are video-derived elementary claims, not
  imported proofs from papers linked in the descriptions.
- **Independent primary witness source:** Andrew V. Sutherland, *Sums of cubes*,
  https://math.mit.edu/~drew/sumsofcubes.html (joint results with Andrew R. Booker;
  mutable page consulted 2026-10-28, no immutable upstream Git identity claimed).
  The large witness for three and the witness for 42 are joint results. The 33
  identity is also given in the original video's March 2019 description update.
  The historical descriptions of 33 and 42 as unsolved are not current premises.
- **Exact integer statement:** `Representable n` means there exist unrestricted
  integers `x,y,z` with `x^3+y^3+z^3=n`. Every integer cube has remainder 0, 1 or 8
  modulo 9; hence a representable integer has neither remainder 4 nor remainder 5.
  The reduction modulo nine is universal, including negative integers. The
  unrestricted sufficiency assertion is only the proposition definition
  `Conjecture`; it is neither an axiom nor a proved implication here.
- **Explicit identities:** the witnesses are
  `(8866128975287528,-8778405442862239,-2736111468807040)` for 33,
  `(-80538738812075974,80435758145817515,12602123297335631)` for 42, and
  `(569936821221962380720,-569936821113563493509,-472715493453327032)` for 3.
  Two smaller triples for 3 are `(1,1,1)` and `(4,4,-5)`; their first coordinates
  distinguish all three displayed ordered triples. This is not completeness,
  optimality, a computation-time claim or an unbounded search theorem.
- **Exact taxicab statement:** positive natural pairs `(1,12)` and `(9,10)` are
  individually increasing and different, and both sums of cubes equal 1729.
  `HasTwoRepresentations` excludes duplicates caused solely by swapping a pair.
  No theorem here states that 1729 is the least such integer.
- **Local paths/blobs:**
  `GRWTSK/NumberTheory/DiophantineEquations/SumsOfThreeCubes.lean`,
  blob `9dc4da6fb7cd3ae134bf5cddc73256d66a623bf3`;
  `GRWTSK/NumberTheory/DiophantineEquations/Taxicab.lean`,
  blob `d2baea4abbb3dd2581fb653d5a44d925539bfcaa`.
  The containing publication commit supplies the complete source tree.
- **Fully qualified declarations:** prefix
  `GRWTSK.NumberTheory.DiophantineEquations.SumsOfThreeCubes.` with
  `Representable`, `Admissible`, `Conjecture`, `cube_residue`,
  `representable_admissible`, `not_representable_of_residue_four`,
  `not_representable_of_residue_five`, `identity_33`, `representable_33`,
  `identity_42`, `representable_42`, `identity_3_large`, `representable_3_large`,
  `three_distinct_solutions_for_3`; prefix
  `GRWTSK.NumberTheory.DiophantineEquations.Taxicab.` with
  `HasTwoRepresentations`, `identity_1729_first`, `identity_1729_second`,
  `two_representations_1729`.
- **Evidence roles:** imported mathematics is Mathlib integer congruence and
  proof-producing arithmetic tactics under the existing dependency pins. New
  kernel-verified bridges: none claimed. Source proof candidates: the fourteen
  named theorems. Conditional interfaces: no new hypotheses disguising an
  unproved theorem; `Conjecture` separately names the missing sufficiency claim.
  Finite evidence: the displayed witness identities and the nine residue cases
  used within the universal reduction, with explicit transport from every
  integer via `Int.mod_modEq` and `Int.ModEq.pow`. Analogies: none. Unresolved
  premises: source elaboration, fresh kernel replay, transitive axiom auditing,
  full video claim extraction, taxicab minimality, three-cubes sufficiency,
  and all other video claims not mapped by this batch.
- **Verification:** compilation NOT_RUN; fresh kernel checking NOT_RUN;
  transitive axiom auditing NOT_RUN. Intended source-bound commands are
  `lake env lean GRWTSK/NumberTheory/DiophantineEquations/SumsOfThreeCubes.lean`
  and `lake env lean GRWTSK/NumberTheory/DiophantineEquations/Taxicab.lean`,
  through a supported Noe managed replay at the publication commit, followed by
  all fourteen theorem audits. The old toolchain issue grwtsk/noeaaeue#408 is
  closed; this does not establish a replay of these new bytes. No native replay
  or host-resource mutation was dispatched. Root imports and dependency pins

## Erdős proof routes: #138 kth-root growth, #242 residue reduction, #1 counting obstruction

Local attribution: grwtsk contributors. This increment distinguishes an imported
complete proof route from two elementary partial-result routes. None has a fresh
local compilation, kernel replay or axiom audit at publication.

### Problem #138: transport an actual upstream proof to the positive-interval definition

- **Source title/byline/version:** OpenAI, *Quantitative Superexponential Bounds for van der Waerden Numbers*, September 23, 2026, pinned `openai/math` commit `7ef5c0cc5ca4b0e072b7a050fca09ab24bc6fa3a`. Stable source: https://github.com/openai/math/blob/7ef5c0cc5ca4b0e072b7a050fca09ab24bc6fa3a/preprints/Quantitative-Superexponential-Bounds-for-van-der-Waerden-Numbers-September-23-2026/paper.pdf . Catalogue question: Thomas Bloom, https://www.erdosproblems.com/138 .
- **Exact imported theorem:** `OAI.QuantitativeVanDerWaerden.kthRoot_tendsto`, for each fixed natural color count `r ≥ 2`, asserts `Tendsto (fun k : ℕ => (W r k : ℝ) ^ (1 / (k : ℝ))) atTop atTop`. Its proof body in `lean/OAI/Combinatorics/ProgressionColoring/Main.lean` invokes the cyclic-coloring construction and its growth theorem. Main blob: `d8ca9d7ce4f064bf9573aefb9e1e136063d2d991`; Model blob: `996e314a8a8293eaaadba41fe40e8522152b8656`. The `lean/docs/160.md` scope note includes growth limits but excludes sharper intermediate paper estimates. The comparator's `sorry` theorem is not the imported proof.
- **New exact definitions/bridge:** positive interval `1,...,N`; arbitrary `c : ℕ → Fin 2`; progression origin `a > 0`, step `d > 0`, endpoint `a+(k-1)d ≤ N`, monochromatic for every `j < k`. `W k` is the infimum of positive `N` with that property. Shifting a coloring by one proves equivalence with upstream's zero-based interval; the sets of admissible positive `N` and their infima are equal. The final theorem specializes the actual upstream limit to `r=2`. No analytic estimate is hidden in a new premise.
- **Local fully qualified declarations:** `GRWTSK.Combinatorics.OpenAIMath.Erdos138.IsRamsey`, `.W`, `.isRamsey_iff_upstream`, `.W_eq_upstream`, `.erdos_138` (each suffix uses that full namespace).
- **Local path/blob:** `adapters/openai-math/GRWTSK/Combinatorics/OpenAIMath/Erdos138.lean`, blob `d3fb08df277f1269760e82d140e1949d70bef2c7`; the containing publication commit pins the full tree. It stays in the existing opt-in provider package, with its committed OAI/Mathlib/Lean pins and no default-root promotion.
- **Mathematical/evidence roles:** imported mathematics is the complete upstream kth-root limit; local source proof candidates are the interval equivalence, equality of Ramsey numbers, and specialization to the catalogue question. Kernel-verified bridges: none claimed locally. New conditional interfaces: none in the theorem statement. Finite evidence: an upstream source scan found no `sorry`, `admit` or top-level `axiom` in the ProgressionColoring directory; this is not a transitive audit. Analogies: none. Unresolved premises are local elaboration, provider/asset closure support, kernel checking and transitive axiom policy; no claim is made about the ratio `W(k+1)/W(k)` variant, novelty, or acceptance of all OpenAI results.
- **Verification:** source inspection performed using `git show` at the above immutable commit. Local adapter compilation `NOT_RUN`; source-bound fresh kernel replay `NOT_RUN`; `#print axioms` for the three local theorems `NOT_RUN`. The maintained Noe route currently rejects the main math toolchain against its provisioned compiler policy (Noe issue [#408](https://github.com/grwtsk/noeaaeue/issues/408)); this adapter additionally needs explicit support for the opt-in package root and OAI's `lean` subdirectory dependency. No unreviewed runner or dependency substitution is permitted. A proposed command is `lake env lean GRWTSK/Combinatorics/OpenAIMath/Erdos138.lean` from the materialized provider package, through a supported source-bound Noe adapter.

### Problem #242: multiplicative transport and a residue-one-modulo-24 reduction

- **Source statement/byline/version:** Thomas Bloom, *Erdős Problem #242*, https://www.erdosproblems.com/latex/242, consulted 2026-10-28; mutable catalogue, with no immutable web blob claimed. Exact statement and earlier families are recorded below.
- **Local path/blob:** `GRWTSK/NumberTheory/DiophantineEquations/ErdosStraus.lean`, blob `a087a095b8a674f55e9dd2cd3dd90fc0ece0e40e` at this increment; publication commit supplies the full tree. This does not replace the earlier blob or retroactively verify it.
- **New fully qualified declarations:** namespace `GRWTSK.NumberTheory.DiophantineEquations.ErdosStraus`, theorems `representation_mul`, `representation_three_mul_add_two`, `representation_eight_mul_add_five`, `conjecture_iff_residue_one_mod_twentyfour`.
- **Exact witnesses/proof:** any representation scales by a positive integer `k`, multiplying all three ordered denominators by `k`. For `n=3k+2`, `k≥1`, use `(k+1,n,n(k+1))`; for `n=8k+5`, `k≥0`, use `(2(k+1),n(k+1),2n(k+1))`. Multiples of three inherit the representation `(1,4,12)` of three. Together with the earlier even and `3 mod 4` families, natural-number residue arithmetic leaves only `n ≡ 1 mod 24`. These are alternative constructive routes; the residual universal statement remains a necessary joint premise for concluding the full conjecture.
- **Roles/verification:** source proof candidates only; imported mathematics is Mathlib arithmetic. Kernel-verified bridges: none claimed; finite evidence and analogies: none. Compilation, fresh kernel replay and all nine named theorem axiom audits: `NOT_RUN`. The actual managed Noe preparation at the earlier pinned source exited 1 with `ValueError: math/compiler toolchain mismatch`, before any Lean compilation. Retained diagnostics live outside math; [math #1431](https://github.com/grwtsk/math/issues/1431) and [Noe #408](https://github.com/grwtsk/noeaaeue/issues/408) distinguish preparation failure from mathematical failure. The remaining residue class is not proved by this reduction.

### Problem #1: count distinct subset sums before seeking a stronger estimate

- **Source/byline/version:** Thomas Bloom, *Erdős Problem #1*, https://www.erdosproblems.com/1, consulted 2026-10-28; mutable catalogue with no immutable web blob claimed. The source states the elementary `2^n/n` counting lower bound. The stronger proposed absolute-constant exponential bound is kept as a proposition definition, with no claim about its current external resolution status.
- **Exact hypotheses/consumer:** `A : Finset ℕ`, all subset sums distinct including the empty sum; if every element is at most `N`, then `2^A.card ≤ A.card*N+1`. The full statement additionally requires nonempty `A` and positive elements, and quantifies one positive real constant independently of both `A` and `N`. Nonemptiness prevents the false empty-set, `N=0` boundary case. The partial counting theorem itself needs neither positivity nor nonemptiness.
- **Proof route:** inject the powerset into `range (sum A + 1)` using subset summation, giving `2^A.card ≤ sum A + 1`; then bound the total sum by `A.card*N`. The missing factor `A.card` cannot be deleted by this argument. A stronger concentration or additive-structure estimate is an additional mathematical obligation, not a premise concealed in a wrapper.
- **Local path/blob/declarations:** `GRWTSK/NumberTheory/AdditiveNumberTheory/DistinctSubsetSums.lean`, blob `4128530ca26874505217aeab1758b383137d68ea`; namespace `GRWTSK.NumberTheory.AdditiveNumberTheory.DistinctSubsetSums` with `SumDistinct`, `Conjecture`, `pow_card_le_total_sum_add_one`, `pow_card_le_card_mul_bound_add_one`.
- **Roles/verification:** imported mathematics is Mathlib finite powersets, cardinality of an injective image, and monotonicity of natural-number sums. New source proof candidates are the two counting inequalities. Kernel-verified bridges: none claimed; conditional interfaces, finite computation and analogies: none used. Local compilation, fresh kernel replay and both axiom audits are `NOT_RUN`, pending supported Noe toolchain admission. Intended command: `lake env lean GRWTSK/NumberTheory/AdditiveNumberTheory/DistinctSubsetSums.lean` through Noe at the publication commit. This is not a proof of the full absolute-constant statement.

## Erdős Problem #242: distinct-denominator Erdős–Straus representations (first increment)

- **Source/byline/version:** Thomas Bloom, *Erdős Problem #242*, catalogue statement consulted 2026-10-28; https://www.erdosproblems.com/latex/242 and https://www.erdosproblems.com/242. Problem attributed to Paul Erdős and Ernst G. Straus. This entry imports the catalogue statement, not a paper's proof or a claim of novelty. The web statement is mutable; no immutable upstream commit/blob is available for this consultation.
- **Exact statement:** for every natural integer `n > 2`, there are natural integers `0 < x < y < z` with `4/n = 1/x + 1/y + 1/z` in the rationals. Strict distinctness and the lower bound on `n` are retained.
- **Local source:** `GRWTSK/NumberTheory/DiophantineEquations/ErdosStraus.lean`, blob `cd3f50c44df66fb49d11b5a28509b99cc877c2ed`; base math commit `ad2ef5597215827580d7c48ef0b81fcf92996cb0`. The containing publication commit supplies the complete source tree; this blob pins the mathematical file independently of this reference entry.
- **Fully qualified declarations:** prefix `GRWTSK.NumberTheory.DiophantineEquations.ErdosStraus.` followed by `Representation`, `Conjecture`, `representation_two_mul`, `representation_four_mul_add_three`, `representation_of_even`, `representation_of_mod_four_eq_three`, and `conjecture_iff_residue_one`.
- **Exact elementary methods/hypotheses:** for `n = 2k`, `k ≥ 2`, use `(k, k+1, k(k+1))`; for `n = 4k+3`, `k ≥ 0`, put `a = (4k+3)(k+1)` and use `(k+1, a+1, a(a+1))`. Positivity, strict ordering, and the rational identities have source proof bodies. These families cover all `n > 2` except residue one modulo four. Their consumer is the equivalence reducing `Conjecture` to that remaining residue class.
- **Imported mathematics:** Mathlib rational arithmetic, natural division/remainders, ordered arithmetic and tactics, under the existing repository dependencies; no upstream Erdős–Straus proof is imported.
- **Kernel-verified bridges:** none claimed. **Source proof candidates:** the five named theorems above. **Conditional interfaces:** the reverse direction of `conjecture_iff_residue_one` requires representations for every `n > 2` congruent to one modulo four. **Finite evidence:** none used. **Analogies:** none asserted.
- **Unresolved premises/bridges:** the entire remaining residue class; source elaboration, fresh kernel checking, and transitive axiom audit. `Conjecture` is a definition of a proposition, with no axiom, `sorry`, or asserted general solution. This increment does not resolve Problem #242.
- **Verification:** compilation `NOT_RUN`; fresh kernel checking `NOT_RUN`; transitive axiom auditing `NOT_RUN`. Requested exact-source command: `lake env lean GRWTSK/NumberTheory/DiophantineEquations/ErdosStraus.lean`, followed by fresh kernel validation and `#print axioms` for all five theorem declarations, managed in `grwtsk/noeaaeue` against the published math commit/blob. No validation execution or successful receipt is claimed. Root imports and dependency pins are unchanged.

Entries separate imported mathematical facts, new source-level proof candidates,
conditional interfaces, finite evidence, analogies, and unresolved hypotheses.
A source file, an inspected proof term, and a queued validation request are **not**
substitutes for a pinned Lean compilation, fresh kernel check, and transitive axiom
audit. Operational runs and receipts belong outside this mathematics repository.

## OpenAI (2026): QAOA attains the SK ground-state energy in the thermodynamic-first limit

- **Upstream title/byline/version:** *QAOA attains the SK ground-state energy in the thermodynamic-first limit*; OpenAI; September 25, 2026 preprint.
- **Stable source:** https://github.com/openai/math/blob/1581d4cbdd69a0faf34b2089614fe2a814316a6a/preprints/QAOA-attains-the-SK-ground-state-energy-in-the-thermodynamic-first-limit-September-25-2026/QAOA-attains-the-SK-ground-state-energy-in-the-thermodynamic-first-limit-September-25-2026.pdf
- **Upstream immutable identity:** `openai/math` commit `1581d4cbdd69a0faf34b2089614fe2a814316a6a`; theorem-statement source `preprints/QAOA-attains-the-SK-ground-state-energy-in-the-thermodynamic-first-limit-September-25-2026/build/sections/01-introduction.tex`, blob `453caa0efc98c73518eb85f3c05bf03743b22e8b`.
- **Source results and exact limitations:** The manuscript's `thm:main` asserts `lim_{p→∞} Q_p = P_*` for zero-field Gaussian Sherrington–Kirkpatrick spins with Hamiltonian `h_{n,J}(σ) = n^{-1/2} Σ_{i<j} J_{ij} σ_i σ_j`, iid standard Gaussian `J_{ij}`, and deterministic fixed QAOA angles selected independently of `n` and `J` before the thermodynamic limit. Its `cor:regular-maxcut` concerns fixed-depth circuit expectations on sufficiently high-girth `(D+1)`-regular graphs (`girth > 2p+1`) and successive random-regular limits, with cost angles `2γ/√D`; it is not an all-graph optimality claim.
- **Method used locally:** Only the **finite classical** relation between an Ising `±1` spin difference, an edge-cut indicator, and the oriented squared-difference energy. The manuscript's MaxCut cost is `H_MC(G) = 1/2 Σ_{\{u,v\}∈E}(I-Z_u Z_v)`; no QAOA, Gaussian or Parisi theorem is imported as a proved local dependency. The elementary factor-eight calculation is a source-level bridge proposed here, not an upstream Lean theorem transported into this repository.

### Non-diagonal finite QAOA operator-kernel support and one-edge Heisenberg conjugation (fifth bounded increment)

- **Upstream paper/title/byline/version:** OpenAI, *QAOA attains the SK ground-state energy in the thermodynamic-first limit*, September 25, 2026 preprint. The stable immutable paper URL, upstream commit `1581d4cbdd69a0faf34b2089614fe2a814316a6a`, and exact manuscript statement source/blob are recorded in this section above. Method used: finite cost-gate factorization, locality of a cost edge under Heisenberg conjugation, and eventual restriction of fixed-depth edge observables to causal cones. The asymptotic QAOA theorem and its tree/SK transfer are **not** imported or proved by the present increment.
- **Exact finite hypotheses:** A finite vertex type `V` with `[Fintype V]` and `[DecidableEq V]`, Boolean configurations `Configuration V := V → Bool`, and an arbitrary complex matrix kernel `K : (Configuration V → Configuration V → ℂ)`. A declared support `S : Finset V` satisfies **both**: (1) `K σ τ=0` whenever `σ v≠τ v` for any `v∉S`; (2) `K (flipAt v σ) (flipAt v τ)=K σ τ` for any `v∉S`. These are the explicit local tensor-factor entry conditions; the second condition prevents hidden dependence on external spin labels. They do not silently assume diagonal operators, normalization, self-adjointness, or a pre-existing abstract operator-support API.
- **Composition/source theorem:** Define `kernelComposition K L σ τ := ∑ρ, K σ ρ * L ρ τ` over the **entire** finite configuration carrier. If `K` and `L` have supports `S` and `T` respectively, the composition is supported on `S∪T`. Proof candidate uses pointwise vanishing for off-support transitions and reindexing the finite complex sum by the spin-flip involution for simultaneous-outside-flip invariance. This statement is finite matrix-kernel multiplication, not a physical locality bound for an arbitrary previously undefined quantum operator.
- **Single-edge conjugation/source theorem:** For arbitrary real `γ` and an edge-endpoint pair `e : V × V`, `edgePhaseConjugate γ e K σ τ := Complex.conj (edgePhase γ e σ) * K σ τ * edgePhase γ e τ`. If `K` is `S`-local, this entrywise `U_e† K U_e` kernel is `S ∪ {e.1,e.2}`-local. If `e.1∉S` and `e.2∉S`, then **exact equality of kernels** `edgePhaseConjugate γ e K = K` follows using the off-support zero condition and the unit-modulus edge phase. The cost edge is the one-count-per-undirected-edge MaxCut edge phase of the existing `QAOAFiniteLocality` representation, not an oriented-energy factor-eight phase.
- **New fully qualified declarations:** All under `GRWTSK.CombinatoricsAndDiscreteMathematics.GraphTheory.QAOAOperatorSupport`: `Kernel`, `IsLocalKernel` with fields `zero_of_outside` and `flip_pair`, `local_mono`, `local_add`, `kernelComposition`, `kernelComposition_local`, `diagonalKernel`, `diagonalKernel_local`, `edgePhaseConjugate`, `edgePhaseConjugate_local`, `edgePhaseConjugate_eq_of_disjoint`. ### Finite Pauli-X mixer kernel and exact non-diagonal Heisenberg support (sixth bounded increment)

- **Upstream source, exact scope, authorship:** OpenAI, *QAOA attains the SK ground-state energy in the thermodynamic-first limit*, September 25, 2026 preprint, upstream `openai/math` commit `1581d4cbdd69a0faf34b2089614fe2a814316a6a`. Immutable manuscript URL and theorem source/blob are recorded above. Imported **method**: finite mixer gates `exp(-iβX_v)` alongside MaxCut cost phases. Neither the manuscript's thermodynamic-first limit nor its Parisi value, rooted-tree transfer, depth bound or seed-removal theorem is claimed to follow here. No historical influence inferred from matching expressions.
- **Exact local carrier and hypotheses:** Finite `V` with `[Fintype V] [DecidableEq V]`; configuration `Configuration V = V → Bool`, amplitudes `State V = Configuration V → ℂ`, matrix kernels `Kernel V = Configuration V → Configuration V → ℂ`, arbitrary real `β` and site `v : V`, and arbitrary finite operator kernel `K`. `IsLocalKernel S K` requires both: (1) every matrix entry vanishes when row/column configurations differ at **any** site outside `S`; and (2) simultaneous row/column spin flips outside `S` preserve each entry. No diagonal, self-adjoint, normalized-state, graph-degree, or girth hypothesis is inserted.
- **Single-site mixer operator:** `mixerKernel β v σ τ` is the complex matrix `cos(β) 1[σ=τ] - i sin(β) 1[σ=flipAt v τ]`. Its source theorem `mixerKernel_local` states `IsLocalKernel {v} (mixerKernel β v)`. `kernelAction` is the entire finite matrix-vector sum. The named source theorem `mixerKernel_action_eq_mixerAt` identifies that matrix action with the **already defined** amplitude-level `QAOAFiniteLocality.mixerAt`, fixing sign and gate ordering without assuming an arbitrary supplied mixer.
- **Physical Heisenberg interpretation and exact algebra:** Source-level `mixerKernel_symmetric` and `mixerKernel_conj_transpose` identify the reverse-angle kernel `mixerKernel (-β) v` with the conjugate transpose of `mixerKernel β v`. The precise two-sided kernel product `mixerKernelConjugation β v K := kernelComposition (mixerKernel (-β) v) (kernelComposition K (mixerKernel β v))` is rewritten by `mixerKernelConjugation_eq_polynomial` as `cos²(β) K + sin²(β) X_v K X_v + i cos(β) sin(β)(X_v K - K X_v)`, expressed entrywise for all `σ,τ`. The proof uses *actual* finite kernel multiplication; no unproved identification with the polynomial is assumed in the theorem statement.
- **Bounded support conclusions:** `mixerHeisenbergPolynomial_local` and `mixerKernelConjugation_local` assert that a mixer at **any** site preserves the *same* `S` support for any `IsLocalKernel S K`, even if `K` is non-diagonal. When additionally `v∉S`, `flip_row_eq_flip_column_of_outside` and `mixerKernelConjugation_eq_of_outside` assert exact cancellation, i.e. the whole conjugated kernel equals `K`. The proof uses `Real.cos_sq_add_sin_sq`, the existing tensor-locality predicate, and site-flip commutation. These are fixed, finite algebraic source-level statements, not a global depth-p QAOA cone.
- **New fully qualified declarations:** All have prefix `GRWTSK.CombinatoricsAndDiscreteMathematics.GraphTheory.QAOAMixerKernelSupport.`. `flipAt_commute`, `flipAt_injective`, `eq_flipAt_iff`, `mixerKernel`, `mixerKernel_local`, `kernelAction`, `mixerKernel_action_eq_mixerAt`, `mixerHeisenbergPolynomial`, `flip_row_eq_flip_column_of_outside`, `mixerHeisenbergPolynomial_eq_of_outside`, `mixerHeisenbergPolynomial_local`, `mixerKernel_symmetric`, `mixerKernel_conj_transpose`, `kernelComposition_mixer_left`, `kernelComposition_mixer_right`, `mixerKernelConjugation`, `mixerKernelConjugation_eq_polynomial`, `mixerKernelConjugation_local`, `mixerKernelConjugation_eq_of_outside`.

### Whole-graph finite QAOA cost-layer Heisenberg locality (seventh bounded increment)

- **Upstream research:** OpenAI, *QAOA attains the SK ground-state energy in the thermodynamic-first limit*, OpenAI, September 25, 2026 preprint. Stable paper URL and source theorem statement with exact hypotheses appear at the beginning of this QAOA section; upstream immutable repository commit `1581d4cbdd69a0faf34b2089614fe2a814316a6a`. The imported *method*, not a proved upstream Lean dependency, is the one-count-per-undirected-edge MaxCut phase `exp(-iγ C_G)` and the fixed-word local tree cone. No historical influence is inferred from mathematical similarity. No SK/Parisi convergence, seeded-control synthesis, or QAOA circuit limit is claimed by this finite source.
- **Exact mathematical carrier/hypotheses:** Finite type `V` with `[Fintype V] [DecidableEq V]`; `G : SpinCutEnergy.SimpleEdgePresentation V` with `G.edges : Finset (V × V)` containing one oriented representative per underlying simple undirected edge, with no loops or reversed duplicate representatives; Boolean configuration `Configuration V := V→Bool`, complex amplitude `State V`, arbitrary complex operator kernel `K : Kernel V`, real phase angle `γ`, finite observable support `S : Finset V`, and the fully specified `QAOAOperatorSupport.IsLocalKernel S K` certificate, comprising (i) vanishing matrix entries when row/column spins disagree outside S, and (ii) invariance under simultaneous row/column spin flips outside S. There is NO degree, girth, randomness, normalized-state, self-adjointness, or Gaussian-limit hypothesis.
- **Exact partition and cost normalization:** `touches S e` iff `e.1∈S ∨ e.2∈S`. `incidentEdges G S := G.edges.filter (touches S)`; `remoteEdges G S := G.edges.filter (fun e => ¬touches S e)`. Each edge is counted exactly once in `incidentCost` or `remoteCost` using `QuantumMaxCut.pauliZEdgeCost`, so `incidentCost G S σ + remoteCost G S σ = SpinCutEnergy.cutSize G σ`, *not* eight times a cut. Their cost phases multiply to `fullCostPhase G γ σ = exp(-i γ cutSize G σ)` with no global-phase slack. The established upstream local definition `QAOAFiniteLocality.incidentClosure G S` includes S and both endpoints of **edges originally touching S**; this is the single spatial expansion counted by the theorem.
- **Exact mathematical step:** If `K σ τ ≠ 0` for an S-local kernel, the spin configurations σ and τ coincide at every endpoint of a remote edge. Thus the remote cost/phase agree between σ and τ and its complex conjugate times itself equals one. The full graph conjugation `fullCostConjugate G γ K σ τ := conj(fullCostPhase G γ σ) * K σ τ * fullCostPhase G γ τ` equals `incidentCostConjugate G γ S K` **on every kernel entry**, not merely in expectation. The incident phase is local to `incidentClosure G S`, proving `IsLocalKernel (incidentClosure G S) (fullCostConjugate G γ K)`. This is non-diagonal operator-kernel support for one full *cost* layer, not a depth-p QAOA circuit bound.
- **Connection to the actual finite operator:** `costLayer_apply_fullCostPhase` and `fullCostPhase_eq_edgePhase_product` identify the full scalar cost with the existing QAOA amplitude gate and the product of its one-edge factors. `costKernel` is `diagonalKernel (fullCostPhase G γ)`; `costKernel_action_eq_costLayer` identifies finite matrix-vector application. `kernelComposition_diagonal_left` and `kernelComposition_diagonal_right` identify diagonal matrix multiplication. `fullCostConjugate_eq_kernelComposition` identifies the full two-sided matrix-kernel product with the conjugate-phase diagonal and the cost kernel. No assertion of a general full-QAOA unitary, graph tree equivalence, or thermodynamic limit is inserted.

### Finite QAOA mixer-sweep, round and depth-indexed causal-cone recursion (eighth bounded increment)

- **Upstream research citation:** OpenAI, *QAOA attains the SK ground-state energy in the thermodynamic-first limit*, OpenAI, September 25, 2026 preprint. The exact title, author, date, immutable paper URL, upstream `openai/math` commit `1581d4cbdd69a0faf34b2089614fe2a814316a6a`, and theorem-statement blob `453caa0efc98c73518eb85f3c05bf03743b22e8b` are recorded in the heading above. Relevant method: cost/mixer gate order, finite light-cone localization, high-girth transfer as a later supplier. No historical influence inferred. The imported *research idea* is not a Lean-verified import of the paper's SK/Parisi result.
- **Exact carrier/hypotheses:** A finite vertex type `V` with `[Fintype V] [DecidableEq V]`, a finite simple undirected edge-representative graph `G : SpinCutEnergy.SimpleEdgePresentation V` with exactly one ordered representative per represented edge (no loops or reverse duplicates), `S : Finset V`, arbitrary `K : QAOAOperatorSupport.Kernel V` and `hK : QAOAOperatorSupport.IsLocalKernel S K`, arbitrary real `γ,β`, a literal ordered site list `vs : List V` or literal ordered circuit angle list `angles : List (ℝ × ℝ)`. Locality requires **both** zero matrix transitions outside `S` and invariance under paired outside flips. No degree/girth, randomness, Gaussian law, symmetry of K, normalized state, or asymptotic hypothesis is added.
- **Gate ordering:** `QAOAFiniteLocality.mixerList β (v::vs) ψ = mixerAt β v (mixerList β vs ψ)`, so the gatewise backwards observable recursion first applies `QAOAMixerKernelSupport.mixerKernelConjugation β v K`, then transforms it through the tail sweep. `mixerLayerHeisenberg` uses exactly `Finset.univ.toList`, matching `mixerLayer`, without assuming order independence. The forward round is `qaoaRound G γ β ψ = mixerLayer β (costLayer G γ ψ)`; backward `qaoaRoundHeisenberg` therefore applies mixer conjugation first, then `QAOACostLayerCone.fullCostConjugate G γ`. The forward list `qaoaCircuit` applies angle pairs by `foldl`, first pair first; the backward observable recursion applies later round updates inside earlier updates. This ordering matters and is not inferred from commutativity.
- **Exact finite mathematical conclusions:** `mixerSweepHeisenberg_local` and `mixerLayerHeisenberg_local` preserve *exactly the same* operator support S for any listed vertices, using the earlier actual two-sided one-site mixer product theorem. If every listed site is outside S, `mixerSweepHeisenberg_eq_of_outside` gives exact equality to K. `qaoaRoundHeisenberg_local` combines mixer preservation and the preceding whole-cost-layer support theorem to produce `IsLocalKernel (incidentClosure G S) (qaoaRoundHeisenberg G γ β K)`. `qaoaCircuitHeisenberg_local` inducts over the angle-list length `p := angles.length` to produce `IsLocalKernel (cone G S p) (qaoaCircuitHeisenberg G angles K)`. No graph-size-dependent circuit parameters occur in this finite theorem; nor does it claim parameters independent of a random SK instance, since there is no random input.
- **Exact edge-observable specialization:** `edgeObservableKernel e := diagonalKernel (edgeCostSymbol e)`. `edgeObservableKernel_action_eq_edgeObservable` connects its finite matrix-vector action to the previously declared edge observable. `edgeObservableKernel_local` proves support at the exact endpoint set `{e.1,e.2}`. The final `qaoaCircuitHeisenberg_edge_cone` proves `IsLocalKernel (cone G {e.1,e.2} angles.length) (qaoaCircuitHeisenberg G angles (edgeObservableKernel e))` for all finite graphs, lists and endpoint pairs; an actual graph-edge membership hypothesis is *not* required to define that pair's cost observable.
- **Definition/representation boundary:** `qaoaCircuitHeisenberg` is an *explicitly defined gatewise Heisenberg recursion*, assembled from previously defined two-sided site-mixer and full-cost matrix-kernel conjugations. The new source does **not** independently construct one full circuit matrix and prove its adjoint-product identity with the previously defined `qaoaCircuit` amplitude fold; that end-to-end correspondence, plus site-mixer order independence, is a named missing bridge. In particular, the final source theorem is not kernel acceptance, a high-girth theorem, an SK energy assertion, or Parisi-optimality.

The existing `kernelComposition A B σ τ := ∑ρ A σ ρ * B ρ τ`, `kernelAction U ψ σ := ∑τ U σ τ * ψ τ`, and finite configuration carrier are reused, not replaced by a parallel matrix theory. ### Exact finite QAOA relevant cost-edge pruning and p-minus-one edge cone (eleventh bounded increment)

- **Paper title/byline/year/version and stable source:** OpenAI, *QAOA attains the SK ground-state energy in the thermodynamic-first limit*, September 25, 2026 preprint. Immutable source: https://github.com/openai/math/tree/1581d4cbdd69a0faf34b2089614fe2a814316a6a/preprints/QAOA-attains-the-SK-ground-state-energy-in-the-thermodynamic-first-limit-September-25-2026 . Upstream `openai/math` commit `1581d4cbdd69a0faf34b2089614fe2a814316a6a`; exact method source `build/sections/08-regular-maxcut.tex` in that preprint, Git blob `3660a4c64a369a6c7e37c58def56d1b29df2824b`, especially the proof paragraph distinguishing relevant cost edges from the larger induced vertex ball. Original theorem/regular-MaxCut method: for fixed depth p, backwards cancellation of cost gates retains only cost edges having an endpoint at distance at most p−1 from the two endpoints of the distinguished edge; an extra edge in the relevant tree cone would close a cycle of length at most 2p+1. The manuscript uses `girth > 2p+1` and (D+1)-regularity in a subsequent regular-tree comparison. **Only the finite pruning/cost-edge counting method is newly formalized here**. Do not infer historical influence from a shared structure or treat the paper's SK/Parisi limit as an imported Lean theorem.
- **Exact local carrier/hypotheses:** `[Fintype V] [DecidableEq V]`, a graph `G : GRWTSK.CombinatoricsAndDiscreteMathematics.GraphTheory.SpinCutEnergy.SimpleEdgePresentation V` with exactly one oriented representative per represented undirected simple edge, no loops, no reversal duplicates, arbitrary finite vertex support `S : Finset V`, arbitrary cost/mixer angle list `angles : List (ℝ × ℝ)`, and arbitrary complex operator kernel `K : GRWTSK.CombinatoricsAndDiscreteMathematics.GraphTheory.QAOAOperatorSupport.Kernel V`. The **exact hypothesis** for operator equalities is `IsLocalKernel S K`, containing BOTH outside-support matrix-transition vanishing and paired-outside-flip invariance. No girth, degree, Gaussian randomness, self-adjointness, normalization, depth rate, or finite-size assumption is required for this finite pruning theorem. The edge-observable case uses the actual `QAOARoundCone.edgeObservableKernel e` with certified support `{e.1,e.2}`.
- **Exact edge set and radius convention:** `relevantCostEdges G S 0 = ∅` and `relevantCostEdges G S (n+1) = QAOACostLayerCone.incidentEdges G (QAOAFiniteLocality.cone G S n)`. Membership is equivalent to **both** `e∈G.edges` and `touches (cone G S n) e`, i.e., at least one endpoint belongs to the ORIGINAL radius-n cone. Hence at depth p>0, relevant cost edges touch the original radius-(p−1) cone; it is **not** the set of all edges induced by the radius-p vertex cone. Every relevant edge lies in the graph and has both endpoints in radius p, but the reverse implication is not asserted. `incidentEdges_mono` and `relevantCostEdges_subset_next` supply the monotonicity needed to compare distinct remaining circuit suffix lengths.
- **All-round cost-edge accounting:** `consultedCostEdges G S angles` recursively unions `incidentEdges G (cone G S rest.length)` for each forward angle pair, where the **tail** rounds act first on the Heisenberg observable. `consultedCostEdges_eq_relevant` proves the *exact equality of finite edge sets* `consultedCostEdges G S angles = relevantCostEdges G S angles.length`, not simply an inclusion. `consultedCostEdges_subset_graph` shows no synthesized/out-of-graph interaction appears.
- **Strongest operator identity:** `relevantCircuitHeisenberg G S angles K` is a new finite, explicitly ordered recursion that uses the actual `QAOARoundCone.mixerLayerHeisenberg` but clips each cost conjugation to `QAOACostLayerCone.incidentCostConjugate G γ (cone G S rest.length)`, where `rest` is the remaining suffix list. For an S-local kernel, `relevantCircuitHeisenberg_eq_full` proves equality to the existing complete `qaoaCircuitHeisenberg G angles K` **at every matrix entry**, by suffix induction, imported `qaoaCircuitHeisenberg_local` and `fullCostConjugate_eq_incidentCostConjugate`. `relevantCircuitHeisenberg_eq_circuitMatrix` further identifies this clipped recursion with `kernelConjugate (qaoaCircuitKernel G angles) K`, using the earlier separately assembled full circuit matrix and its end-to-end Heisenberg identity. The edge specialization `edge_relevantCircuit_eq_fullMatrix` obtains this exact equality for the distinguished MaxCut edge observable without assuming the existence of a tree iso. `relevantCircuitHeisenberg_local` retains the original depth-indexed support bound.
- **Finite boundary evidence, separately classified:** `fourCyclePresentation : SimpleEdgePresentation (Fin 4)` uses edges (0,1),(1,2),(2,3),(3,0). The source theorem `fourCycle_fringe_edge_not_relevant` supplies an explicit finite **source proof candidate**: edge (2,3) belongs to the graph and both endpoints belong to the radius-one vertex cone of the distinguished edge (0,1), yet (2,3) is **not** a relevant depth-one cost edge. This formally distinguishes the correct cost-edge cone from an incorrectly enlarged induced vertex ball. The source does **not** prove a Mathlib `egirth` value for that example, so do not label the example a Lean-verified high-girth graph.

**Mathematical assumptions and exact method.** For naturals q,N, χ:DirichletCharacter ℂ q, sigma:Real with 1<sigma and N>=3, arbitrary real T, and real t for pointwise comparison: s=sigma+i*t and a_n=dirichletVonMangoldtCoefficient q χ n. Mathlib LSeries.term(a,s,N)=a_N/N^s at N!=0, N^s!=0 by pinned Complex.cpow_ne_zero_iff, and s!=0 from sigma>1. Thus term(a,s,N)*(N^s/s)=a_N/s. Define K(sigma,T)=(2*pi)^(-1)*integral_{-T}^T (1/(sigma+i*t)) dt, with inherited oriented real-parameter integral. Then exact term integral is a_N*K(sigma,T) and the endpoint error norm is norm(a_N)*norm(K(sigma,T)-1/2). This yields the genuinely quantitative but nonasymptotic bound norm(finitePerronHeightDefect) <= sum_{n<N} norm(finitePerronTermIntegral(n)-a_n) + norm(a_N)*norm(K(sigma,T)-1/2). In particular K(sigma,0)=0, disproving any unconditional assertion that a finite-height endpoint integral always equals a_N/2. No assumption or proof of K(sigma,T) converging to 1/2 is smuggled into these theorems. No hSeven or global prime error is used.

For EVERY real sigma>0 and ARBITRARY real finite T (negative T is allowed by oriented integration), the new source proof candidate evaluates the full complex integral as the real cast of 2*Real.arctan(T/sigma) and yields K(sigma,T)=((Real.arctan(T/sigma)/Real.pi:Real):Complex).   envelope at every retained depth. It additionally requires, for every δ>0,

  it supplies neither the arithmetic model/residual nor the all-exponent growth
  premise. Fixed-shift qualitative OpenAI convergence supplies neither.

  is a **source proof candidate** for natural-number Diophantine power-sum search:
  n≥3 and a^n+b^n=c^n imply a=0 or b=0, with no positivity premise on a,b,c.

  pinned Anthropic FLT transport. This strengthens the consumer interface to
  include degenerate input/output cases; it is not an independent Wiles proof.

## Union-closed small members and Collatz positive descent

Local attribution: grwtsk contributors. Source commit:
`9cea61d877c8c4089e9274454af886d665acc231`, reachable from `main`.
[Exact-source verification issue #1434](https://github.com/grwtsk/math/issues/1434).
These are elementary formalizations of known partial results and a conditional
reduction, with no claim of novelty or solution of either open problem.

### Union-closed families: singleton and doubleton members

**Source.** Henning Bruhn and Oliver Schaudt, *The journey of the union-closed
sets conjecture*, arXiv:1309.3297v2, 30 October 2013,
https://arxiv.org/abs/1309.3297v2, Sections 4–5. The survey attributes the small-member
observations to D. G. Sarvate and J.-C. Renaud. Supporting source mapping:
Douglas B. West, *Union-Closed Sets Conjecture (1979)*,
https://www.dwest.web.illinois.edu/openp/unionclos.html, partial-results discussion.
Historical priority beyond these explicit source attributions is not inferred.

**Exact object and hypotheses.** `F : Finset (Finset α)` for an arbitrary type
with decidable equality. Distinctness of family members is enforced by `Finset`.
`UnionClosed F` means every union of two members belongs to `F`; the empty set
need not be a member. `frequency F x` counts members containing `x`;
`Abundant F x` means `F.card ≤ 2 * frequency F x`. A singleton hypothesis
`{x} ∈ F` proves abundance of `x`. A pair hypothesis `{x,y} ∈ F` proves abundance
of `x` or `y`, including the degenerate `x = y` case.

**Proof and use.** Union with a fixed set is injective on inputs avoiding every
one of its elements. For a singleton, inject the avoiding subfamily into the
containing subfamily and partition `F`. For a pair, write `a,b,c,d` for the
cardinalities of the both, only-x, only-y and neither classes. Union with `{x,y}`
gives `d ≤ a`. The two frequencies are `a+b` and `a+c`, whose sum is at least
`a+b+c+d = F.card`; hence at least one is at least half. This eliminates singleton
and doubleton members from every counterexample and supplies a reusable counting
consumer. It does not find an abundant element in an arbitrary family.

**Declarations and source identity.** All names below have prefix
`GRWTSK.CombinatoricsAndDiscreteMathematics.SetFamilies.UnionClosedSmallMembers.`:
`UnionClosed`, `frequency`, `Abundant`, `Conjecture`,
`union_injective_on_avoiding`, `abundant_of_singleton_mem`,
`abundant_of_pair_mem`, `no_small_member_of_no_abundant`.
Source: `GRWTSK/CombinatoricsAndDiscreteMathematics/SetFamilies/UnionClosedSmallMembers.lean`,
Git blob `249ee949d9523efddd63875fd6427d0ba4f5a194` at the commit above.

**Evidence roles and obstruction.** Imported mathematics: the pinned Mathlib
finite-set cardinality and partition APIs. New kernel-verified bridges: none yet.
Source proof candidates: the four stated theorem declarations. Conditional
interfaces: `Conjecture` is a proposition, not an axiom or supplied proof.
Finite evidence and analogies: neither is used as proof acceptance. Unresolved:
there need not be a singleton or doubleton in an arbitrary nontrivial family.
Union with `{x,y}` on sets merely avoiding `x` is not generally injective:
for `x ≠ y`, the distinct sets `∅` and `{y}` have the same image. The required
selector or stronger local-configuration argument has not been supplied.
After recording that obstruction, this increment moved to Collatz.

### Collatz: an infinite restricted family and the precise descent premise

**Source.** Terence Tao, *Almost all orbits of the Collatz map attain almost
bounded values*, arXiv:1909.03562v7, 16 July 2026,
https://arxiv.org/abs/1909.03562v7, abstract definition of the unaccelerated map.
The initial version is from 2019 and the journal article appeared in 2022;
this entry pins v7 for its statement identity. No density theorem, probabilistic
method, or quantitative estimate from that paper is imported.

**Exact object, strategy and use.** `step n` is `n/2` for even `n` and `3*n+1`
for odd `n`, extended to zero to make a total natural-number function. `orbit k n`
is exactly `k` steps, and `ReachesOne n` means existence of a finite orbit witness.
`Conjecture` quantifies over every positive starting integer. `PositiveDescent`
requires for every `n > 1` a finite iterate strictly between zero and `n`.
Orbit concatenation prefixes a convergent orbit by a finite segment. A double
halves in one step; induction therefore proves convergence of powers of two
and of `2^k*n` whenever `n` already converges. Strong induction then proves
`Conjecture ↔ PositiveDescent`. The latter is an exact reduction exposing the
missing premise, not an independently discharged convergence argument.

**Declarations and source identity.** All names below have prefix
`GRWTSK.NumberTheory.ArithmeticDynamics.CollatzDescent.`:
`step`, `orbit`, `ReachesOne`, `Conjecture`, `PositiveDescent`, `orbit_add`,
`reachesOne_of_orbit`, `reachesOne_one`, `step_double`, `reachesOne_double`,
`reachesOne_pow_two`, `reachesOne_pow_two_mul`, `conjecture_iff_positiveDescent`.
Source: `GRWTSK/NumberTheory/ArithmeticDynamics/CollatzDescent.lean`,
Git blob `38c4029bd94d8f60cceafc34db5915600bce8266` at the commit above.

**Evidence roles and obstruction.** Imported boundary: pinned Lean core and its
arithmetic tactic, without Mathlib. New kernel-verified bridges: none yet.
Source proof candidates: the eight theorem declarations. Conditional interface:
the descent equivalence retains its universal premise. Finite evidence: no finite
search is claimed. Analogy: none promoted. Unresolved: strict positive descent
for every input above one. An almost-everywhere statement, including one in
logarithmic density, does not imply this universal obligation. No solution of
Collatz is claimed; the next research increment must move to another problem
or produce a new independently supported descent argument.

## Erdős–Moser small exponents and matching-compiler follow-up

Local attribution: grwtsk contributors. [Verification issue #1434](https://github.com/grwtsk/math/issues/1434).

**Source identity.** Bernd C. Kellner, *On stronger conjectures that imply the Erdös-Moser conjecture*, arXiv:1003.1646v2, 5 September 2012, https://arxiv.org/abs/1003.1646v2; journal publication 2011, https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jnt.2011.01.004. Only the abstract's equation is a statement reference; no stronger conjecture, paper theorem or method is imported. These elementary local proofs formalize known restricted cases without a novelty claim.

**Exact hypotheses and use.** `sumPowers k n` sums `1^k` through `n^k`; `Solution k n` means that sum equals `(n+1)^k`. Thus local `n` is the paper's `m-1`. For every natural `n`, `solution_one_iff` states `Solution 1 n ↔ n = 2`, and `not_solution_two` excludes `Solution 2 n`. The full `Conjecture` excludes all `k ≥ 2`, `n > 0`; it is a proposition, not an axiom. These results eliminate the first two exponents before considering the unresolved higher ones.

**Proof strategy and stopping boundary.** The linear sum exceeds the next integer at three summands, and its recursion preserves strict excess; the smaller inputs are evaluated directly. For `t ≥ 3`, the inequality `3*t ≤ t*t` gives `(t+1)^2 ≤ 2*t^2`. At four summands the square sum is `30 > 25`. Adding the next square preserves excess over the following square; induction covers every larger number of summands. The quadratic induction gives no uniform exclusion for `k ≥ 3`. No full open problem is solved.

**Fully qualified declarations and mathematical source pins.** Prefix `GRWTSK.NumberTheory.DiophantineEquations.ErdosMoserSmallExponents.`: definitions `sumPowers`, `Solution`, `Conjecture`; theorem candidates `linear_sum_gt`, `solution_one_iff`, `next_square_le_twice_square`, `square_sum_gt`, `not_solution_two`. Source path `GRWTSK/NumberTheory/DiophantineEquations/ErdosMoserSmallExponents.lean`, commit `682b62ec7cf4928049cc2a24966921c8518db489`, Git blob `5fe982a9ad2ca8f66b5aa781d2ab56695ddc7977`.

**Evidence roles.** Imported mathematics: installed pinned Lean core and its arithmetic tactic. Kernel-verified bridges: none yet. Source proof candidates: the five theorems above. Conditional interface: the full conjecture retains both hypotheses and is not discharged. Finite evidence: explicit small base cases occur inside the proposed inductive proofs; no finite computation is promoted to the universal conclusion. Analogies: none promoted. Missing bridge: all higher exponents, together with source-bound checking of these candidates.

## Collatz checking failure, repairs and source relocation

Local attribution: grwtsk contributors. Continuation of the preceding union-closed,
Collatz and Erdős–Moser source entries; paper identities, exact hypotheses and
mathematical stopping boundaries remain as recorded there.
[Verification issue #1434](https://github.com/grwtsk/math/issues/1434).

The first admitted managed replay, external `research/unsolved-mathematics/core-run-v4`,
actually compiled the original Collatz source at math
`682b62ec7cf4928049cc2a24966921c8518db489` and **FAILED**. It found three diagnostics:
`orbit_add` cannot close its zero case by reflexivity because `0+b` needs an
explicit simplification; `if_pos` is deprecated in Lean 4.34.1; and the specified
`Nat.mul_assoc` simp argument is unused. The latter two are errors under the
reviewed warnings-as-errors policy. Receipt SHA-256:
`209eb310eddbd05605cfeb4f38472ad4c0af5ca6881a89098e83e72efeeff530`. The receipt records `passed=false` and
`host_config_restored=true`; no selected contract completed. Diagnostic axiom
output included compiler-generated `sorryAx` from failed elaboration. That failed
output is rejected, not admitted as a theorem or committed as a proof premise.

The three repairs are published on `main` in
`f6c8eadedaf7703b37dbd3b56acdf372efc88d7c`, current source
`GRWTSK/NumberTheory/CollatzDescent.lean`, Git blob
`2fd3bfd370039cba453fda0a6b8031f299f222c9`. They explicitly simplify `Nat.zero_add`,
use `ite_eq_left` and omit the unused simp argument. Statements and hypotheses are
unchanged. Concurrent repository relocation moved the unchanged other sources to
`GRWTSK/NumberTheory/ErdosMoserSmallExponents.lean` (blob
`5fe982a9ad2ca8f66b5aa781d2ab56695ddc7977`) and
`GRWTSK/Combinatorics/UnionClosedSmallMembers.lean` (blob
`249ee949d9523efddd63875fd6427d0ba4f5a194`). All fully qualified declaration
namespaces in the earlier entries remain unchanged. These path mappings do not
transfer acceptance from historical source to repaired bytes.

Fresh managed preparation succeeded for `core-profile-v5.json` (Collatz and
Erdős–Moser) and `union-closed-profile-v5.json`, against the repaired source commit.
Together they select all seventeen earlier theorem names and explicitly bind
all seventeen expected theorem types. The first repaired-source retry,
`core-run-v5`, exhausted a 60-second activation wait after another queued owner
obtained admission; no Lean child ran in that retry. Its failure streams remain
retained. The successor `core-run-v6` uses the maintained CLI's explicit bounded
activation wait of 21600 seconds and the same immutable core registry. It grants
no lock preemption, resource change or mathematical acceptance.

Actual replay command, from the Noe checkout:

```text
python3 -B managers/formalization/lean/environment/python/managed_contract_replay.py run --registry <external-runtime>/research/unsolved-mathematics/core-registry-v5.json --output <external-runtime>/research/unsolved-mathematics/core-run-v6 --reuse-enabled-idle-host --activation-wait-seconds 21600
```

`<external-runtime>` denotes Noe's selected sibling runtime. Exact profiles,
registries, logs, receipt and continuation live there; `increment-03.json` retains
current paths, source pins and the active attempt identity. Repaired-source
compilation, kernel replay, declaration-scoped transitive axiom audits and
expected-interface consumers are **PENDING / NOT_RUN until admission and actual
receipts**. The union-closed profile is prepared but not dispatched in this
increment. Source-level repairs and runtime waiting do not upgrade acceptance.
The fifteen-minute heartbeat monitors the existing attempt without starting a
duplicate, prioritizes actual proof diagnostics, then proceeds to union-closed
checking. No new mathematical bridge or full open-problem solution is claimed.

## Johnston–Yang: complete coprime small-cofactor accounting

**Imported source.** Daniel R. Johnston and Andrew Yang, *Some explicit estimates for the error term in the prime number theorem*, arXiv:2204.01980v2, 20 April 2022 (document date 21 April), https://arxiv.org/pdf/2204.01980v2; DOI https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jmaa.2023.127460. Corollary 1.3 equation (1.6), page 2, all real x>=2: |pi(x)-li(x)|<=9.59 x(log x)^0.515 exp(-0.8274 sqrt(log x)); unshifted principal-value li from0. Primary pages1–2 inspected. No historical influence inferred.

**Local sources.** grwtsk contributors. Extended documentation/mathematics/NumberTheory/Moebius/prime-cofactor-negative-family.md, blob ef4864fc3dccc37eddb7ceb56b3d61aa9ae6cca0; new documentation/mathematics/NumberTheory/Moebius/complete-small-cofactor-family.md, blob f25eaf6626855e64814001bca8e93ce4ba7c0d33. Inspection base 57a2cad10d185dbb52890b96f5c98e04242b712c; source commit is the containing main commit. Notes pin exact source conversation/message/rendered-final hashes. Original N=2^j, frozen Gram/horizon, strict gap, terminal endpoint and gcd parameter d<=2^ceil(j/8) retained. Small cofactor primes j^2<ell<=j^8 avoid d; both squarefree cofactors are completed and coprime, exactly one prime>j^8 per residual core, with both large primes avoiding d and each other. Explicit sufficient inequalities, eventually valid uniformly in d, give a positive proper-family bound of order a(d)^2 N/(d^2(log N)^2), and the uniform asymptotic a(d)^2 N/(d^2 log^2(N/d))*(c2 J1/2+o(1)). Both source tails and the independently derived shared-large-prime correction are priced. No numerical starting threshold is certified.

**Classification and declarations.** Imported written mathematics: the pinned scalar PNT estimate only. New local written source proof candidates: finite-degree sign reversal, complete double-cofactor product plus actual-source Rankin comparison, shifted-log and full prime-error estimates, positive-family bound/asymptotic. Existing coefficient GRWTSK.NumberTheory.Moebius.PickupPrimeBlockSource.rough and stage selection GRWTSK.NumberTheory.Moebius.PickupGrowingPrimeStages.stages. **Verification and precise missing bridges.** Compilation NOT_RUN; fresh kernel replay NOT_RUN; transitive axiom audit NOT_RUN. No proof execution dispatched. ## 3Blue1Brown: elastic collision algebra and mass-weighted energy (first increment)

- **Scope/source:** Grant Sanderson, *How colliding blocks act like a beam of light...to compute pi.*, 2019-02-03, https://www.3blue1brown.com/lessons/clacks-via-light/ ; text adaptation credited by the source to Josh Pullen. Consulted 2026-10-28. The lesson is mutable; no immutable upstream web blob is claimed. The larger request includes all videos, Shorts, guest videos, talks and appearances elsewhere. No complete video or exhaustive inventory is claimed.
- **Local source pin:** `GRWTSK/Mechanics/Classical/ElasticCollision.lean`, blob `84e61612c43ddd36438cbc76481cc890d1c58995`; base math commit `4b0ff1e76e1b2b9b295562ca8554d4748249eef2`; containing publication commit pins the complete tree.
- **Exact objects:** real masses m,M and velocities u,v; first updated velocity `((m-M)u+2Mv)/(m+M)` and second `((M-m)v+2mu)/(m+M)`. Algebraic identities assume only `m+M ≠ 0`. The square-root coordinate bridge additionally assumes both masses nonnegative. Physically positive masses satisfy these joint premises; the source does not model finite-size blocks, contact times or trajectories.
- **Fully qualified declarations:** prefix `GRWTSK.Mechanics.Classical.ElasticCollision.` with definitions `first`, `second`; theorem candidates `momentum`, `twice_energy`, `relative_velocity`, `first_involution`, `second_involution`, `equal_mass_first`, `equal_mass_second`, `wall_twice_energy`, `scaled_energy_eq`, `scaled_energy_conserved`.
- **Proof and mathematical use:** clear the nonzero mass-sum denominator, then use polynomial identities. The update preserves momentum and twice kinetic energy, reverses relative velocity and is an involution. Equal nonzero masses exchange velocities. The coordinate map `(u,v) ↦ (sqrt(m)u,sqrt(M)v)` identifies twice kinetic energy with squared radius and transports energy conservation to that radius. This supplies an explicit representation bridge for a future geometric collision count, rather than assuming that bridge as a field.
- **Boundary:** conservation alone also permits the unchanged state; a collision law must select the nontrivial reflection. Zero total mass is excluded from the collision identities. Negative masses are excluded from the real square-root bridge. Wall reflection preserves energy but does not conserve the momentum of the two-block subsystem.
- **Evidence roles:** imported mathematics is Mathlib real arithmetic, square roots and tactics under existing pins. All ten local theorems are source proof candidates; kernel-verified bridges: none claimed. Conditional interfaces: no assumed collision-count theorem. Finite evidence and analogies used as proof: none. The optics interpretation is not yet a proved equivalence of trajectories.
- **Missing bridges:** uniqueness/physical selection of the collision update; admissible contact dynamics and wall events; mass-weighted reflection law; unfolding with event-count preservation; exact angle and rounding conventions; collision count and decimal digits of pi. These are distinct mathematical obligations. Coverage of the three-video sequence, let alone every video, remains incomplete.
- **Verification:** compilation `NOT_RUN`; fresh kernel checking `NOT_RUN`; transitive axiom auditing `NOT_RUN`. Proposed source-bound command through supported Noe tooling: `lake env lean GRWTSK/Mechanics/Classical/ElasticCollision.lean`, followed by fresh replay and `#print axioms` for each named theorem. No execution dispatched. Static discovery inventory: `documentation/mathematics/_verification/targets/three-blue-one-brown-source-inventory.json`; its 177 official lesson URLs are pending review, not 177 formalized videos. Root imports and dependency pins are unchanged.


## 3Blue1Brown colliding blocks: physical events, reflection and strict angular rounding

- **Sources/bylines:** Grant Sanderson, *The most unexpected answer to a counting puzzle* (2019-01-13), https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=HEfHFsfGXjs ; *Why do colliding blocks compute pi?* (2019-01-20), https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=jsYwFizhncE ; *How colliding blocks act like a beam of light...to compute pi.* (2019-02-03), https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=brU5yLm9DZM . Official lesson pages are https://www.3blue1brown.com/lessons/clacks/ , https://www.3blue1brown.com/lessons/clacks-solution/ and https://www.3blue1brown.com/lessons/clacks-via-light/ . The first and third credit Josh Pullen for text adaptation. The later *Why colliding blocks compute pi* (2025-03-13), https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=6dTyOl1fmDo , https://www.3blue1brown.com/lessons/colliding-blocks-v2/ , is included as a separate source version and credits Josh Pullen for text adaptation. Its date and upload identity were read from the official structured metadata. Video IDs were read from the official lesson HTML on 2026-10-28, not inferred from titles.
- **Paper:** G. Galperin, *Playing Pool with π (the Number π from a Billiard Point of View)*, 2003, DOI https://doi.org/10.1070/RD2003v008n04ABEH000252 , public PDF https://www.maths.tcd.ie/~lebed/Galperin.%20Playing%20pool%20with%20pi.pdf . Exact methods are Lemma 2(c) and sections 8–10: the integral angle endpoint, unfolding/count reduction, reciprocal-arctangent approximation, and the subsequent arithmetic questions/conjecture. The front-page digit theorem is not imported without the later qualifications. No claim is made about the present external resolution of that conjecture.
- **Source pins:** `GRWTSK/Mechanics/Classical/ElasticCollisionDynamics.lean`, blob `5572c411b021650b556f36c63ccb19e52a12f4ca`; `GRWTSK/Mechanics/Classical/CollisionAngleCount.lean`, blob `6df098b099eb624a3fa9b6418e850628c8c03195`. The containing publication commit supplies the complete source tree. The imported local `ElasticCollision.lean` remains blob `84e61612c43ddd36438cbc76481cc890d1c58995`, first published at `abe554299af332650b82c482aeab7b1401a1555d`.
- **Exact physical objects/hypotheses:** order bodies as near-wall/light and far-wall/heavy; point positions satisfy `0 ≤ x ≤ y`. Free flight advances positions at unchanged real velocities. An isolated event requires nonnegative time, admissible start, strictly contact-free open flight, the appropriate endpoint contact, strict incoming velocity and positive distance from the simultaneous-impact vertex. The event constructors do not silently assert general existence or deterministic next impact. Momentum and relative-velocity reversal uniquely determine the collision map when `m+M ≠ 0`. Square-root reflection uses both masses nonnegative and nonzero total mass. No physical claim for negative masses follows from an algebraic identity.
- **Physical declarations:** prefix `GRWTSK.Mechanics.Classical.ElasticCollision.`; `collision_law_unique`, `square_mass_reflection_first`, `square_mass_reflection_second`, `scaled_reflection_first`, `scaled_reflection_second`, `State`, `State.advance`, `State.admissible`, `State.openFlight`, `State.energy`, `Event`, `Step`, `advance_energy`, `step_energy`, `step_admissible`, `blocks_leave_separating`, `wall_leaves_outgoing`, `after_blocks_next_wall`, `after_wall_next_blocks`, `Trace`, `trace_energy`, `no_step_of_outgoing`, `equal_mass_three_impact_run`.
- **Proof/use:** the linear collision equations give uniqueness. In squared-mass coordinates polynomial identities give twice the parallel projection minus the input; substituting the nonnegative square roots returns that reflection to the original masses. Event constructors enforce contact and direction, imply nonpenetrating endpoints, preserve twice energy, and force alternating event types whenever a next event exists. Trace induction transports energy. The equal-mass theorem explicitly constructs three contact-free flights and impacts from `(x,y,u,v)=(1,2,0,-1)` to `(1,1,0,1)` and proves no subsequent event is possible; it does not yet cover arbitrary initial separations/speeds or establish uniqueness of all traces.
- **Exact angular objects/declarations:** prefix `GRWTSK.Mechanics.Classical.CollisionAngleCount.`; `angularCount θ = ceil(pi/θ)-1` as an integer; `le_angularCount_iff`, `angularCount_bounds`, `angularCount_eq_iff`, `angularCount_nonnegative`, `angularCount_error`, `angularCount_pi_div_four`, `massAngle`, `massAngle_pos`, `massAngle_lt_pi_div_two`. The mass angle is `arctan(sqrt(m/M))`, positive for positive masses. For positive θ, an integer n is the angular count exactly when `n*θ < pi ≤ (n+1)*θ`. The approximation is `0 < pi-N*θ ≤ θ`; this is not decimal-floor agreement.
- **Boundary/use:** ceil minus one handles the grazing integral endpoint: θ=pi/4 gives three, whereas an unconditional floor formula gives four. The angular model is independent of physical `Trace`; the missing event-count correspondence is not hidden as a theorem premise. Conservation does not select collision over unchanged velocities. Event alternation does not prove existence, termination or a count. A small angular approximation error does not by itself establish exact decimal digits.
- **Evidence roles:** imported mathematics is Mathlib arithmetic, square roots, real arctangent and integer ceiling under existing dependencies; the 23 new theorems are source proof candidates. Kernel-verified bridges: none claimed. Conditional interfaces: no assumed physical/angular count equivalence and no assumed all-exponents digit theorem. Finite evidence: the normalized three-impact example is represented by explicit source proof terms, still unchecked. Analogies as proof: none; the projection-reflection identity is explicit algebra, while a full equivalence of trajectories remains unproved.
- **Verification:** both new modules: compilation `NOT_RUN`, fresh kernel checking `NOT_RUN`, transitive axiom audit `NOT_RUN`. Intended supported Noe checks are `lake env lean GRWTSK/Mechanics/Classical/ElasticCollisionDynamics.lean` and `lake env lean GRWTSK/Mechanics/Classical/CollisionAngleCount.lean`, with named axiom checks and independent exact-type consumers for the three-impact run and angular interval characterization. A managed fresh-closure run of the ten original declarations at `abe554299af332650b82c482aeab7b1401a1555d` has been dispatched, but no terminal acceptance is asserted here; operational attempts and receipts remain outside math. Pending source-bound verification is tracked in https://github.com/grwtsk/math/issues/1439 .
- **Next mathematical bridge:** prove a deterministic first-impact/existence theorem for admissible initial states, then connect the alternating physical trace to a straight unfolded ray and the strict angular count. General termination/count, the demonstrated unequal-mass counts, parameter scaling, reciprocal-arctangent error bounds, and justified decimal statements remain open locally. None of the four video sources is marked fully formalized.

Galperin, *Playing Pool with π (the Number π from a Billiard Point of View)*, 2003, DOI https://doi.org/10.1070/RD2003v008n04ABEH000252 , https://www.maths.tcd.ie/~lebed/Galperin.%20Playing%20pool%20with%20pi.pdf , Lemma 2(c) and section 8. Grant Sanderson, 2019 colliding-blocks sequence, especially https://www.3blue1brown.com/lessons/clacks-via-light/ (text adaptation by Josh Pullen). Folder heading is official MSC2020 70F35, *Collision of rigid or pseudo-rigid bodies*, under *Mechanics of particles and systems / Dynamics of a system of particles, including celestial mechanics*. Classification attribution remains Mathematical Reviews and zbMATH; this is a subject assignment, not proof acceptance. Declaration namespaces remain `GRWTSK.Mechanics.Classical.ElasticCollision`. The concurrent global MSC2020 layout map has not yet been published on main; this new file needs addition to that map after its publication, with actual source hashes. Existing source relocation and rewritten imports retain their separate old/new byte identities.
- **Deterministic trace declarations:** namespace prefix `GRWTSK.Mechanics.Classical.ElasticCollision.` with `step_deterministic`, `Terminal`, `terminal_of_outgoing`, `initial_block_step`, `terminal_trace_length_unique`. Same-kind contact equations give a unique flight time; for competing kinds a midpoint after the earlier putative contact violates the later strict open flight, and equal times violate positive distance from the vertex. Deterministic first steps plus terminality give equal lengths for any two complete traces. Initial positions satisfy `0<a<b` and speed `c>0`; the first block Step is explicitly constructed at `(b-a)/c`. This construction does not assume that a subsequent impact exists.
- **Unfolding objects:** prefix `GRWTSK.Mechanics.Classical.ElasticCollision.Unfolding.` with `side`, `boundary`, `crossingTime`, `evenX`, `evenY`, `oddX`, `oddY`, `gap`, `evenState`, `oddState`. The unfolded affine ray is `(A-W*t,H)`; a ray boundary is `H*cos(alpha)-(A-W*t)*sin(alpha)` and its crossing time is `(A-H*cos(alpha)/sin(alpha))/W`. Nonzero/positive sine and speed hypotheses are retained. Crossing-time differences are `H*sin(beta-alpha)/(W*sin(alpha)*sin(beta))`; positive height and `0<alpha<beta<pi` give strict ordering.
- **Unfolding theorem candidates:** `boundary_at_crossing`, `boundary_affine`, `boundary_pos_iff`, `boundary_neg_iff`, `crossingTime_sub`, `crossingTime_strictMono`, `even_wall_side`, `even_block_side`, `odd_wall_side`, `odd_block_side`, `wall_switch`, `block_switch_first`, `block_switch_second`, `massAngle_cos`, `massAngle_sin`, `massAngle_cos_two`, `massAngle_sin_two`, `massAngle_gap`, `reflected_heavy_velocity`, `reflected_light_velocity`, `first_crossing_time`, `outgoing_of_weighted_sides`, `terminal_even_sides`, `terminal_odd_sides`, `counted_terminal_sides`, `evenState_advance`, `oddState_advance`, `interior_of_weighted_sides`, `even_segment_openFlight`, `odd_segment_openFlight`.
- **Mathematical use/hypotheses:** the fold maps retain signed wall and block gaps exactly. Sine/cosine of `atan(sqrt(m/M))` are derived for positive masses; geometric heavy-first reflection is then identified with the actual physical heavy/light collision outputs, with the coordinate swap explicit. The signed gap is a positive scalar times `y-x` (and similarly `v-u` for velocities). Both terminal parities give outgoing velocities for `N=ceil(pi/theta)-1`, including the integral grazing boundary. Folded affine states obey `State.advance`, and the open-flight theorems prove genuine strict noncontact throughout each interval between consecutive crossings. They do not take physical/angular event-count equivalence as a premise.

New source proof candidates: 34 public theorems plus six private helpers; private elaborated names are not claimed before compilation. Kernel-verified bridges: none claimed. Analogies as proof: none.
